#6711. What Is an Upper Bleph? Here’s What to Know About the Eyelid Surgery

Doctors explain how the procedure works, when a brow lift may be needed, and nonsurgical options.

In the world of plastic surgery, the nose, breasts, and butt have commanded a lot of time and attention under the knife. But as tech advances and patients seek out more subtle ways to manage aging skin, doctors have shifted their focus to other areas, some once deemed too risky to touch. Blepharoplasty, or bleph, for short, is a plastic surgery procedure that trims, redistributes, or removes excess skin on the eyelids.

The procedure can be done to help improve peripheral vision or for more cosmetic purposes, like removing hooded eyelids. Blephs can be done on the eyelid itself, which is known as an upper bleph, or a lower bleph, which is done below the eye to treat puffy or saggy skin. Even the most effective eye creams and potent skin care formulas sometimes may not be enough to correct excess skin around the eyes, but before you schedule your consultation, see what doctors have to say about the procedure to determine if it’s the best option for you right now.

An upper bleph specifically addresses the eyelid, where skin and sometimes fat closest to the nose are removed. Dr. Lyle Leipziger, chief of plastic surgery at North Shore University Hospital and LIJ Medical Center in New York, explains that the procedure involves removing skin and excising or repositioning deeper fatty tissue to address droopiness, puffiness, and under-eye bags. “As we age, the skin around the eyes naturally loses elasticity, which can cause the upper eyelids to droop, look heavy, or create a tired appearance. This can also be exacerbated by excess upper eyelid fatty tissue,” says Leipziger. During surgery, he explains, excess skin is removed, and surgeons may also remove or reposition small amounts of fat to create a smoother, more defined upper eyelid contour. “The goal is to open up the eyes and create a brighter, more refreshed appearance while preserving the patient’s natural eye shape,” he adds.

Dr. Lesley Rabach, a board-certified facial plastic surgeon and co-founder of Rabach Aesthetics in NYC, says that the best candidates for an upper bleph are people who have a “normal” eyebrow position with excess skin, also known as dermatochalasia. “When considering an upper bleph, your surgeon needs to look at the eyebrow position, eyelid skin, and fat, as well as how much the eye opens when determining if an upper bleph is the correct procedure,” she notes. Rabach says that sometimes a brow lift and/or ptosis repair—which reconstructs the eyelid muscles or connects it to the forehead muscle to lift it—is part of the surgery as well.

Additionally, Leipziger adds that blephs are ideal for individuals who have excess skin or fullness in their upper eyelids that makes their eyes look heavy, tired, or aged. “This is especially common as we get older and lose elasticity in the delicate skin around the eyes,” he points out. “Ideal candidates are generally in good overall health, have realistic expectations, and are bothered by drooping upper eyelid skin and possible fatty tissue.” He recommends having a consultation with a board-certified plastic surgeon to determine whether overhanging skin, excess fatty tissue, brow position, or another anatomical factor is contributing to the appearance of the upper eyelids or if it’s something else.

Rabach says the results are both subtle and obvious at the same time. “They remove the extra skin that often has a crepey texture while restoring the youthful appearance of the eyelid,” she says. “An upper bleph should restore the natural youthful architecture of the upper eyelid that is natural to the person.”

By removing excess skin and, according to Leipziger, when appropriate, repositioning small amounts of fat from the upper eyelids, the procedure helps create a more open and defined eye area. “Patients should experience a significant improvement with their eyes looking brighter and less tired, without ever looking ‘done’” says Leipziger. He also notes that while an upper bleph usually lasts a long time, it doesn’t slow down or stop the way your skin will naturally age. So you may see some sagging or loss of elasticity return over time. “The goal is a natural-looking enhancement rather than a dramatic change in appearance,” Leipziger expresses.

The eye area is very delicate, but blephs are less invasive than you’d think. Rabach performs the procedure at her practice several times a week using just local anesthesia. “However, we use very thin stitches that stay in place for close to one week and must be removed. While there is little to no pain, it’s hard to camouflage the stitches, so we tell our patients they will feel perfectly fine to work from home even after the surgery that day, but not be working from the office,” says Rabach.

Leipziger agrees that recovery from a blepharoplasty is generally minimal compared to other cosmetic surgeries. He says most patients experience some swelling, bruising, and mild discomfort during the first week, which gradually improves. “I typically recommend taking about five to seven days off from work and social activities for an upper blepharoplasty, and a little longer when a lower blepharoplasty is performed,” says Leipziger, adding that most people can resume normal activities within two weeks.

For long-term eye lifting and opening, blephs are the way to go, but Leipziger says several nonsurgical treatments can help improve the appearance of the upper eyelids—even if only temporarily. It all depends on your specific concerns. “Botox can provide a subtle brow lift by relaxing specific muscles, which can help open up the eye area. Certain laser and radiofrequency treatments can also improve skin quality and mild skin laxity by stimulating collagen production and tightening the skin. These options can be beneficial for patients with early signs of aging or minimal excess skin,” says Leipziger. The biggest difference is that the nonsurgical options cannot remove excess eyelid skin or fat. “For patients with significant skin laxity or hooding, an upper blepharoplasty is typically the most effective solution for achieving a more open, refreshed appearance,” Leipziger clarifies.

According to Rabach, a fat transfer is another option to restore hollowness along the orbital bone. This technique can help smooth crepey texture that can set in with age. She also recommends lasers such as CO2 to help improve skin quality, but specifies they aren’t a replacement for surgical removal of excess skin.

As previously mentioned, eyelid skin—even if there’s less of it—will continue to age after an upper bleph. “Long-lasting results come from general health and care of the area. Always wear sunscreen and wear sunglasses to protect the skin, as it’s the thinnest skin on the body and very delicate and prone to sun damage,” says Rabach.

Along with the non-negotiable sunscreen, Leipziger recommends medical-grade skin care products and ingredients such as retinol or peptides to support skin quality. “Additionally, patients may benefit from complementary nonsurgical treatments, such as Botox, laser skin rejuvenation, and/or chemical peels. While surgery cannot stop the aging process, proper skin care and maintenance can help patients enjoy their refreshed appearance for many years,” Leipziger concludes.

#6710. Cashmere Walls, Baby Goats, and a Bruce Springsteen Sighting at Loro Piana’s Cashmere of the Year Award

The invitation to Tuesday evening’s Loro Piana dinner arrived via courier. It was printed on heavy card stock, made out in gold ink calligraphy and sealed with red wax. It was no run-of-the-mill invite, which these days mostly arrive via email or DM, nor was it to announce the usual celebration, like a new shoe or bag. Instead, it celebrated farmers and their fibers—in particular, the farmers hailing from Mongolia who act as the conduit behind the brand’s beloved cashmere clothing and the fibers they reap from their flocks of goats in the mountains.

Now in its 11th year, the Loro Piana Cashmere Award spotlights the finest in cashmere fiber production, and that is meant in the most literal sense. At The Brant Foundation in the East Village, the first sight that greeted guests was three bundles of soft white fiber, illuminated with a dramatic spotlight. These were the winning lots selected for their superior quality, and cultivated by Mr. Siqingbilige and Mrs. Hasiqiqige, a husband-and-wife duo in first place, followed by Mr. Jiyatu and Mrs. Gegentuya in second place, and Mr. Lifuqiang and Mrs. Qiqi in third place. “The herdsmen bring us about 10 million kilos of sheared fiber, and this is the best 100 kilos, which we celebrate for their fineness and length of the fiber,” Pier Luigi Loro Piana, the son of the brand’s founder, Franco Loro Piana, told the room. “That denotes the quality.”

In addition to his role as the company’s deputy chairman, he is widely credited with creating the “baby goat cashmere” that thrust Loro Piana into the upper echelons of luxury when it was first revealed in 2008. “It took me many years to convince the herdsmen not to blend the 3% of baby cashmere fiber with the rest of the older goats,” he recalled. “It required extra work, but the difference was so clear, and it is this measure of quality that creates wealth for this population and attracts younger herdsmen to join them.”

In the room were many Loro Piana loyalists, including Huma Abedin, Gucci Westman, Jeff Koons, and Jessica Springsteen, the accomplished show jumper who serves as an ambassador for the brand’s equestrian line. She was joined by her father, the legendary rocker Bruce Springsteen, who was gleefully received by a room largely filled with Italian, French, and American guests. Evidently, the appeal of The Boss is universal.

Also on hand was Jeremy Strong, who has been outfitted by Loro Piana both on and off screen many, many times, though his first memory of the brand predates his career. “I have a memory of being in Italy and seeing the chicest man I’d ever seen in my life with two wolfhounds in a vintage Maserati convertible, and I saw the Loro Piana red mark on the back of his collar, so that image has forever emblazoned itself in the aspirational sky of my mind—just wanting to be that guy in that car,” he told Vogue.

For this occasion, Strong sported a monochromatic suit in a precise shade of Loro Piana brown-beige, and went on to tour the exhibition that had been built to highlight the farmers and their bounty. It included one room with walls of voluminous, fluffy strands of cashmere fiber which ballooned and wafted toward guests walking by. It quickly began clinging to the arms and shoulders and heads of many partygoers before an army of dashing men would offer to remove it with lint brushes with glossy tortoise handles. (In Loro Piana land, even the lint catchers are a considered detail.)

“I think what I was struck by upstairs walking through the exhibition was the way Loro Piana achieves such specificity by hand in this age of mechanical reproduction, this age where everything is computers and AI, and everything is mechanized and done by a machine,” Strong later added. “There is this care, and tactile beauty, and sort of sweat equity that goes into creating these garments. I find something very moving about that.”

The evening’s dinner—beef carpaccio, a plate of saffron risotto, and a fig tart—set the stage for further reminiscing about Loro Piana vintage finds and making plans to visit Mongolia to see the goats and the herdsmen firsthand, followed by an announcement.

After all these ceremonies celebrating the finest 100 kilos of cashmere fibers, something would finally be made using all of these fibers. “We have kept these winning fibers for the last 11 years, and we will be making a collection using them,” Frédéric Arnault, the company’s CEO, told the room. “It will be very small quantities.”

Pier Luigi Loro Piana and Laura Loro Piana

#6708. Everything We Know About Coachella 2027

It’s the most wonderful time of the year: Coachella has officially dropped the list of performers for its 2027 festival, and as usual, it offers a stacked lineup across genres, with power players in pop, alternative, R&B, and more on the bill.

The headliners include Dua Lipa, SZA, and Tame Impala. Madonna will also be at the festival following the release of her most recent album, Confessions II, but will perform in the dance tent instead of a main stage—a cheeky nod to the exact same billing she received when she performed at the festival as the first dance act in 2006.

Here’s everything we know about Coachella 2027 so far, should you plan on trekking to the desert next spring.

The 2027 festival will once again take place at the Empire Polo Club in Indio, California. Coachella will run from April 9 to 11 and April 16 to 18, 2027.

In addition to Dua, Madonna, SZA, and Tame Impala, other performers include Zara Larsson, Jisoo, Kehlani, Kelela, Haute & Freddy, Phoebe Bridgers, Robyn, Adéla, Tiffany Day, Underscores, Becky G, Babymonster, Muna, Magdalena Bay, Shygirl, Victoria Monét, Caroline Polachek, and many more.

Passes for Coachella go on sale on November 13 at 12 P.M. PT. There will be different tiers—from GA to VIP and more—as well as options that include shuttle transportation to and from the festival. Passes for both weekends can be purchased here, with more information available here.

Not heading to the desert this year? No problem—YouTube plans to partner with the festival again as the exclusive livestream partner for both weekends of Coachella. Fans can watch performances on the official YouTube channel.

#6706. We’re in a James Bond Fever Dream Now

“Next James Bond ‘REVEALED,’” reads a headline published by The Sun last week. “After a loose-lipped co-star blabs secret after ‘a few lagers.’” The article suggests that Josh O’Connor, who has recently been named in the wider press as a last-minute hopeful in the race to find the next Bond actor, is the shoe-in to be 007—all based on what one of O’Connor’s cast mates in the theater production Golden Boy might have maybe told a stranger while drunk at The French House in London.

“They said that he had confided in them about getting the role when the cast of Golden Boy had celebratory drinks after the play finished its run on Halloween,” the article continues. So, there it is: we have our next Bond! Source: someone told someone else down the pub. No, really, honest!

This is nothing against O’Connor, mind. As Ben Allen argued in the latest edition of GQ’s Intermission newsletter, he would make for a killer, surprising pick, suggestive of an “actually new direction” delineating from the hyper-machismo that defined Bond through to the end of the Craig era. His characters tend to be quieter and more introspective; his performance style is similarly subtle. (The hipster choice? Maybe, but obviously a great one.) But the article is a symptom of how utterly insane the maelstrom of reports around the next Bond have become as we seemingly enter the casting endgame, with screen tests reportedly taking place in October. Further, producer Amy Pascal has said that we can probably expect an announcement to come before the end of the year.

Over the past weekend, Robert Pattinson and Nicholas Hoult emerged out of nowhere as having taken part in those aforementioned screen tests. Both would’ve come as whiplash to anyone who has had a passing interest in the endless Bond casting conversation: sure, they’ve been in and around the pack over the years, but given neither are called “Callum Turner” or “Jack Lowden,” the only speculated names taken seriously for much of 2026, they’re big—but both welcome!— shocks. I’m as much of an RPatz supremacist as anyone else who likes to think they know ball about films, the actor routinely being the best thing about his projects, from Nolan’s Tenet and all of his zany arthouse movies to both The Odyssey and Primetime this year. Much love to our man Hoult too, an underrated cornerstone of franchise cinema writ large, from X-Men to the new Superman. But still: what the hell?

In their own article about Pattinson and Hoult joining the race, Variety might’ve actually buried the most baffling lede: according to them, Harry Styles has “had discussions about the part,” while they also note that The Gentlemen’s Theo James—long thought out of the running—is actually yet to be ruled out. No shade on Styles, but he hasn’t been in a film since he briefly dipped a toe in acting in 2022’s Don’t Worry Darling and My Policeman, neither of which exactly kickstarted his movie career. And, like, he’s Harry Styles. Bond song? Yeah, we can see the vision. But the idea of him actually being 007 feels more like the product of an ayahuasca fever dream.

Regardless, Styles’s suitability for the role isn’t really the point. What’s more insane is that the Bond casting conversation only seems to have hit a level of psychosis as it nears the finish line: every other hour, the reported frontrunner switches from Lowden to Turner to O’Connor or Pattinson, a fever pitch of speculation and rumor-mongering that was tired before it was tired. Gary Oldman reckons it should be Lowden! Pierce Brosnan backs Turner! As one person astutely put it on Threads: “OH MY GOD JUST PICK A JAMES BOND. DO ANY JAMES BOND, JUST JAMES BOND A JAMES BOND.”

But it’s not just Bond himself, it seems. As I sat down to write this piece, an email hit my inbox from the publicist for a betting company. Subject line: “Maya Jama odds slashed to star in the next Bond film.” Damn it. Just bring back Pierce.

This article originally appeared in British GQ.

#6705. See the First Teaser for the Final Season of ‘Emily in Paris’

Au revoir, Emily! Darren Star’s frothy Netflix romp will be ending with its sixth season. Here’s everything we know so far.

Yes—watch the first official teaser here.

With Emily now back in Paris, everything’s to play for. Will she answer Gabriel’s (Lucas Bravo) postcard and head to Greece? (It’s looking like it, yes!) Now that she’s broken up with Marcello (Eugenio Franceschini) for good, is that prior relationship now the endgame? Will Mindy (Ashley Park) actually marry Nico (Paul Forman)? If not, will she at least have a Big Fat Greek bachelorette in Mykonos? (The answer to this also seems to be yes, per the first stills and teaser.) And after that, will she just end up getting back together with Alfie (Lucien Laviscount)? And on the work front, what will become of Sylvie’s (Philippine Leroy-Beaulieu) beloved Agence Grateau now that Princess Jane (Minnie Driver) has moved in?

The official log line for the next season reads: “Stepping into a new era of ambition and romance, Emily faces defining choices that test her ultimate loyalties. But as unexpected career conflicts and shifting relationships force her to reevaluate where her true future lies, she must make one final choice between her career, her heart, and the life she’s built in Paris.”

“I wanted the final season to feel like the biggest Emily in Paris experience yet,” Star added in a statement. “We went to multiple locations this season, and Greece was somewhere we really wanted to showcase in a spectacular way. That’s part of the fantasy of Europe: you’re always within reach of another extraordinary place, another culture, another adventure. I wanted the audience to have that vicarious experience of traveling right along with Emily.” We can hardly wait.

After the fifth season finale, we know that the aforementioned Driver is sticking around, as well as the rest of the ensemble: Samuel Arnold (Julien), Bruno Gouery (Luc), and William Abadie (Antoine Lambert).

Netflix has now revealed that the final season will be arriving sooner than we might have expected: on December 24, to be precise. Mark your calendars now.
